市場焦點
美國
• 10月S&P Global綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖較前月回落，但仍處高檔區間；服務業動
能略為放緩，製造業PMI則升至56.0，創近4年半新高。新訂單增速加快，顯示企業需求、投
資及庫存回補動能仍具韌性，然而企業售價與投入成本同步上升，反映需求改善、供應鏈延
遲及成本轉嫁效應持續存在，通膨降溫進程仍具不確定性。整體而言，市場關注焦點轉向通
膨與利率走勢，高利率環境恐維持較長時間，進而推高股債資產評價波動。
中國
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期，在出口與科技製造業帶動下，
短期成長可望維持穩定，但若內需未能有效改善，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散，建議聚焦在
半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈，及高端製造相關產業，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股，

仍需觀察後續刺激政策與消費信心回升情況。
法國
政局動盪
• 法國金融市場因各地抗議活動持續擴大，面臨股債雙跌的局面。市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支
出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴
大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標仍處於歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐
元區主權債務危機期間，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務危機的風險仍相對有限。

美國PMI維持強勁擴張，經濟成長續航但通膨壓力再升溫
• 企業需求強勁支撐美國景氣維持高檔擴張：美國10月綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖略低於前月，但仍處於近五年來
的高位區間，顯示企業活動持續高速擴張；其中服務業PMI為57.9、製造業PMI升至56.0並創53個月新高，新訂單增速
更創逾4年半最佳表現，反映內需穩健、出口需求改善與企業補庫存效應同步發酵，美國第四季經濟動能仍具韌性。
• 成本上升與供應瓶頸推升通膨風險：企業售價漲幅創2022年8月以來最高，能源、運輸、金屬原料及薪資成本同步上揚，
加上供應鏈交期延長與訂單積壓擴大，使企業得以持續轉嫁成本，暗示未來數月消費者通膨可能再度偏離聯準會目標區
間，市場對利率維持較高水準的預期恐因此升溫。
• 後市觀察：景氣韌性有利獲利成長，但通膨可能推升殖利率、壓抑高評價資產。投資配置建議優先布局美國大型科技股、

美國金融股及工業資本支出受惠族群，可透過NASDAQ 100 ETF、S&P 500 ETF及美國銀行股ETF參與企業獲利成長；
固定收益則以短天期投資級債為主，以降低利率波動風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.24
S&P Global PMI 走勢

海外資金流入美國市場，支撐美國資產吸引力
• 海外資金持續流入美國：美國財政部8月TIC資料顯示，
整體資金淨流入1,998億美元，其中民間部門淨流入
1,785億美元，為主要資金來源；外資淨買進美國長
期證券3,007億美元，遠高於美國投資人加碼海外資
產216億美元，反映全球資金在經濟成長與流動性考
量下，對美國金融市場仍維持高度的配置需求。
• 全球資金偏好美國資產：外資8月淨買入美國股票
1,319億美元，高於美國公債與公司債，顯示海外投
資人仍看好美國企業獲利與經濟基本面，雖然短期國
庫券持有量下降，部分跨境美元資金亦出現波動，但
以10月近況來看長天期公債標售仍吸引境外央行、
保險資金及大型資產管理機構參與，反映高殖利率水
準對長期資金仍具吸引力。在經濟成長維持韌性與企

業獲利支撐下，美國資產仍為全球資金配置的重要核
心市場。
• 市場看法：海外資金流入有助反映美國資產吸引力，
惟後續仍須觀察通膨、Fed政策及企業獲利表現。投
資布局建議可以美國投資等級債、美國中短天期公債
及大型AI基礎建設受惠族群為核心配置，兼顧收益、
防禦及長期成長潛力。

顧
歷史數據顯示全球股市突破歷史新高後，後續上漲機率高
• 很多人直覺以為見高就是頂，但過去20年的統計資料顯示，全球股市在創下新高後，多數時間選擇繼續向上走。
• 勝率顯著偏高：全球股市(MSCI全球指數)創新高後持有一年，獲得正報酬的機率高達62%，三個月後的正報酬機率更達
71%。美股與台股表現最亮眼，標普500指數創高後一年上漲機率為76%，那斯達克為79%；而台灣股市表現更為驚人，創
高後三個月內上漲的機率高達82%。
• 下檔拉回風險有限：在創高後短期(一個月內)修正幅度其實相當溫和，全球多數主要市場的回徹幅度中位數僅1%～2%左右。
• 突破前高優於連續創新高：若市場在歷經至少一個月以上的整理後突破前高，其未來一年的平均報酬率高達8.4%，遠高於
連續每天創新高的3.9%。這代表市場經適度整理後的突破，具備更健康的上漲動能。

創新高後的平均遠期報酬率
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22

顧
市場寬度收窄，由少數巨頭獨撐
• 當前的市場特徵由少數巨頭帶頭衝：科技股權重較高的指數(如那斯達克、台股、韓股)，長期以來都有少數權重股拉動指數
的現象。自2024年以來，全球13個主要市場中有10個市場在創新高時，參與上漲的股票比例明顯下降。已開發市場創新高
時高於200日均線的股票比例從過去75.5%降至65.1%，新興市場更降至61.3%。這意味著指數看似風光，但多數個股並未
同步創高，當漲勢過度集中於少數大型科技巨頭時，整體市場的抗風險能力會相對減弱。
• 小結與投資建議：歷史經驗顯示，創新高本身是強勢市場的特徵，後續繼續走揚的機率遠大於立刻崩盤。然而，鑑於目前市
場寬度收窄、權重高度集中於科技股的現狀，盲目追高個股的風險已顯著增加。投資建議可以利用ETF掌握大盤趨勢，由於

指數上漲主要由龍頭股驅動，透過市值型ETF(如追蹤標普500ETF)能直接享受龍頭巨頭帶來的指數創高紅利，避免選錯個股
而賺了指數、賠了價差。此外，提防短期股市回檔，可運用定期定額分批布局或等待大盤適度拉回整理再行加碼，降低追高
風險。
創新高時不同水準的市場寬度佔比
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22
已開發市場和新興市場創新高時的寬度

顧
法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓
• 法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓：法國各地抗議活動持續擴大，最初由學生發起，隨後因生活成本高漲而引發其
他群體響應。在財政空間有限、俄烏戰爭與美伊衝突推升能源價格的背景下，政府難以有效緩解民生成本壓力，法國金融市
場因此面臨股債雙跌的局面。
• 公債殖利率攀升，但系統性風險仍相對可控：市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10
年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標（CISS）仍處於
歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐元區主權債務危機期間，顯示金融市場尚未出現廣泛的系統性壓力，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務
危機的風險仍相對有限。
G7社會動盪指數，法國高居榜首

資料來源： Beco models country，2026.10.19 資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.19

資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.15 資料來源： tradingeconomics，2026.10.21
日本貿易收支
• 日本貿易逆差擴大，進口增幅高於出口：日本2026年9月貿易逆差擴大至5,366億日圓，較去年同期的2,777億日圓增加93%，
高於市場預期的1,810億日圓，連續第五個月呈現貿易赤字。出口年增16.7%至10.97兆日圓，受惠於半導體相關電子零件、
製造設備及汽車出口成長；惟進口年增18.9%至11.51兆日圓，增幅高於出口，主要受到原油、半導體電子零件及液化天然
氣進口增加帶動。其中，原油進口年增51.1%，部分反映日本在荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張下，積極分散能源採購來源。
• 日本央行本週料維持利率不變：日本央行9月將政策利率調升25個基點至1.25%，創31年來新高。考量前幾次升息對經濟的

傳導效果仍待觀察，加上貿易逆差擴大反映進口成本壓力，多數分析師預期日本央行本週將暫停升息，以評估貨幣緊縮對經
濟與通膨的影響。

中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期：顯示中國經濟在第二季落底後呈現溫和回升，從季度表現來看，
一、二、三季增速分別為5.0%、4.3%及4.5%，反映政策支持與外需復甦開始發揮效果，全年達成官方4.5%成長目標的機率
明顯提升，第三季經濟改善的主要動能來自出口，受惠於全球企業持續擴建AI基礎設施，及科技產品需求強勁，中國高科技
產品出口表現亮眼，尤其是積體電路、伺服器、電腦零組件，及儲存設備等產品增幅顯著，高技術製造業維持雙位數成長。
同時，中國對美國及歐盟出口維持強勁，推升貿易順差走升，為支撐經濟的重要力量。然而，內需疲弱仍是當前經濟最大隱
憂，房地產調整尚未結束，居民消費與企業投資意願保守，使經濟復甦結構呈現「外熱內冷」的特徵。

• 展望後市：在出口與科技製造業帶動下，短期成長可望維持穩定，但若內需未能有效改善，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散，建
議聚焦在半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈，及高端製造相關產業，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股，仍需觀察後續刺激政
策與消費信心回升情況。
資料來源：開源證券，2026.10.22

彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高
• 彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高：彭博產業研究認為，中國AI產業未來將由大型科技平台主導，其中騰
訊、字節跳動與阿里巴巴憑藉龐大用戶基礎、數據資源、資金實力及完整生態系統，在AI競賽中具備明顯優勢，這些企業可將
AI廣泛應用於電商、社群、遊戲、金融與雲端業務，提升營運效率並強化競爭門檻，例如：阿里巴巴利用 AI 驅動電商平台上的
個人化產品推薦，而騰訊則運用 AI 優化短影音廣告並強化微信功能。騰訊自 2026 年 7 月起開始將混元 AI 模型整合至其電子
遊戲中，使遊戲角色及助理能更準確地回應玩家，然而，AI模型訓練與基礎建設投入龐大，獲利能力短期仍受壓抑，且須面對
監管、資料隱私及晶片供應等挑戰。此外，智譜、月之暗面、MiniMax及商湯等AI新創雖具技術創新能力，但缺乏規模效益、

穩定現金流及長期資本支持，未來獲利挑戰較大。
• 投資建議：看好中國AI長期發展者，可優先關注具資金、算力與應用場景優勢的大型平台企業，受惠程度可望高於多數AI新創
公司。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.21

台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高，台股第四季有望先蹲後跳
• 台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高：9月外銷訂單1,142.1億美元，連兩個月破千億美元，再創單月新高；年增率高達62.4%，連續
20個月呈雙位數正成長。經濟部表示，AI強勁需求超乎預期，預估今年第四季接單還會優於第三季。展望10月表現，外銷
訂單將持續亮麗，估落在1,125億至1,159億美元，有望連三個月破千億美元。
• 台股第四季有望先蹲後跳：10月初以來，台股在盤中觸及歷史高點49,968點後面臨一波修正，主要反映在新一輪的財報公
布前，市場觀望AI投資是否存在泡沫化風險。短期來看，台股在季線附近出現支撐，不過在11月初的美國期中選舉落幕前，
台股維持區間震盪的可能性較高；中期來看，美國期中選後往往因政治不確定性下降，歷史統計股市上漲機率高，加上台灣

年底集團作帳及選舉行情，正向看待年底台股表現。
資料來源：經濟部統計處，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.20。

2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣(DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

Industry View In-Line
4Q26 iPhone builds unchanged: We maintain our 4Q26 iPhone build forecast at
72M units (+33% q/q, -5% y/y). While builds are expected to increase sequentially,
volumes remain below last year’s level, with softer legacy-model production,
including the iPhone 16/17e, weighing on the outlook. Our 2H26 build forecast for

the new iPhone 18 family remains unchanged at 71M units (-21% y/y), with the y/y
comparison also affected by one fewer SKU launched than last year. Within this, we
continue to expect 7M units of iPhone Duo builds. We will continue to monitor the
production ramp and sell-through data for potential upside or downside to our build
estimates. Our full-year forecast implies CY26 iPhone shipments of 234M units
(+3% y/y), with modest growth for the year despite the softer 4Q y/y outlook.

4Q26 iPad builds unchanged; year-end production outlook remains subdued: We
maintain our 4Q26 iPad build forecast at 10.3M units (-10% q/q, -26% y/y), implying
continued pressure on production volumes into year-end. Our full-year estimate
remains 46.8M units (-14% y/y), with the sharper y/y contraction in 4Q pointing to a
weaker finish to the year.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Allen Chang

Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C.
Aspeed (5274.TWO): Mgmt. visit: BMC and SMC specification migration 
and customers’ expansion to drive growth ahead; Buy
16 November 2026 | 11:05PM HKT
We visited Aspeed in Taipei recently. Management remain positive on future growth, 
riding on further customer expansion, product mix upgrade, and their continuous 
new product launches. The company highlights its comprehensive coverage across 
various chipset platforms (e.g. GPU, ASIC, RISC-V, etc.) and coverage across different 
layers in AI data center (e.g. component-level, server-level, rack-level, data 
center-level), securing their leading market position. We remain positive on Aspeed 
and see the growing servers demand along with AI servers design getting more 
complicated to support the company’s growth ahead. Maintain Buy. Read more: 
Aspeed Oct revenues and 3Q26 results in line. 
Key takeaways 
1. Positive tone on future growth: Management remain positive on future business 
growth, driven by further customer expansion, product mix upgrade, and their 
continuous new product launches. The company’s shipment is mainly from BMC2600 
in 2026, and 1700 and 2700 would further expand shipment in 2027, carrying higher 
ASP as stronger specification (e.g. integrated more functions, I/O supporting DDR5). 
The company would also have 2705 prior to 2800, which offers stronger security. The 
2800 is targeted to start sampling in 2028, and would be a driver beyond 2028, 
integrating more functions, stronger security (e.g. silicon-level security), stronger I/O, 
and using more advanced processing nodes. On SMC and Platform, the company will 
also have new products entering mass production in 2H27: (1) SMC 1040 and 1840 
(embedded FPGA, serving both single-node and multi-node), and (2) platform 1080, 
aiming to further expand customers. The company’s pure exposure to the server 
market also brings it strong end market demand, with the ability to pass through the 
higher raw material costs to end customers (e.g. higher costs from substrate, OSAT, 
foundry), supporting its GM. 
2. Competition advantages: Management highlight they are early in the market and 
serve various customers (e.g. GPU, ASIC, RISC-V, etc.), with comprehensive product 
coverage across different SoC platforms, and the company is also expanding its 
product portfolio vertically: across SMC (component-level mgmt.), BMC 
(server-level), and RMC (rack-level). The company’s products can monitor various 
scenarios, such as GPU thermal issue, liquid cooling leakage, etc. Management also 
highlight their large R&D team (over 100 engineers), local and timely support (having 
R&D team in Taiwan, close to customers), and ecosystem (work closely with ODMs). 
We remain positive on Aspeed’s leading market position and see AI server 
specifications getting more and more complicated (e.g. more shelves, more chips per

### Page 2

rack, from air cooling to liquid cooling, etc.), which would further enhance Aspeed’s 
competition edges. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Aspeed (5274.TWO) 
Valuation: Our 12-month TP of NT$22,000 is based on a target P/E of 40x (in line with 
its 10-year historical trading average) applied to our 2028E EPS, and discounted back to 
2027E at 13.6% CoE. Our key assumptions for CoE include: (1) 1.5x beta (sourcing 
Bloomberg), (2) a 4.25% risk-free rate, and (3) a market risk premium at 6.25% (in line 
with GS house view). 
Key downside risks to our view and TP: 1) A softer-than-expected server market 
demand recovery, 2) slower-than-expected BMC penetration within AI servers, 3) 
intensifying market competition.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 16 Nov 2026 close.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

大銀微簡介：大銀設立於 1997 年 4 月，為上銀的關係企業，產品線分為精密運動
及控制元件、微米與奈米級定位系統兩大類。元件類主要產品有線性馬達、 力矩
馬達、控制器、致動器等。定位平台為模組化產品，為客製化產品。公司與上銀共
同以「HIWIN」自有品牌行銷全球，服務地區涵蓋美國、德國、日本、瑞士、捷克、
以色列等。相關同業如台達、匯川等。 
3Q26 EPS 達 2.14 元，達新高水準：3Q26 營收 8.55 億元（+45.5%YoY），毛利
率達 42.4%，稅後淨利 2.57 億元（+257%YoY）， EPS 為 2.14 元，創下歷史新高；
累計前三季，EPS 為 4.06 元。第三季毛利率上升乃因 stage 出貨佔比增加以及規
模經濟發揮，本季 stage 佔比上升至 58%。 
訂單能見度 2-6 個月，產能持續擴充當中：現階段客戶訂單與出貨進度進展未有
變化，stage 訂單能見度 5-6 個月、交期 3 個月，元件訂單能見度 3 個月、交期
2-2.5 個月，stage 產能擴充第一階段已完成，反映在八月營收突破四億元，第二
階段預計明年 2-3 月完成，4 月可開始貢獻營收，目前已開始規畫第三階段擴廠；
元件明年投入自動化、半自動化機台來提升產能。公司預計今年整體資本支出 1.2-
1.5 億元。 
提升元件比重、stage 進入新應用領域為未來發展方向：現階段元件比重達 40%，
公司長期目標為 45%-50%，大銀微產品搭配上銀傳動元件來擴展市場，元件產品
潛在市場規模是 stage 的十倍，加以可以進行自動化或半自動化量產，規模擴大後
規模經濟效益更明顯。在 stage 產品，公司產品目前應用在非接觸式製程，未來將
進入精密鑽孔、塔式分選、Hybrid Bonding 等接觸式製程新領域；矽光子領域產
品已於 8 月開始出貨，產品應用在 insertion 3 光學引擎測試以及系統及測試，訂
單能見度很高，已可看到 2027 年中，使用公司產品可解決巡光耗時以及微小震動
可能導致光損，此可提升客戶光學檢測效率，與每小時產出量。 
毛利率預計可維持在 40%：在毛利率方面，公司預計第四季可維持在 40%，中長
期目標也在 40%上下，明年新產能開出與新產品、新領域的開拓，加上公司採用
自動化與半自動化提升效率、優化成本結構，毛利率可維持在 40%。 
財務預估： 2026 年 營 收 43.12 億元(+58.8 ％ YoY) ， 稅 後 淨 利 7.16 億元
（+196%YoY），EPS 為 5.98 元。2027 年營收 51.02 億元（+18%YoY），稅後
淨利 8.73 億元（+21%YoY），EPS 為 7.28 元。

Document 220: 7799禾榮科 永豐

### Page 1

個股聚焦  
2026/11/23

陳奕均 fion.chen@sinopac.com

禾榮科 (7799 TT) 
獲利成長列車即將啟航

永豐觀點 
台灣藥證年底取證在即，1H27 起設備陸續商轉，營收進入兌現期，隨全球佈局深
化與自製設備落地，禾榮科技術、平台、生態綜效全面釋放，獲利加速成長期將至。 
 投資評價與建議 
建議買進：禾榮科自 2027 年起將迎來營運轉捩點，正式從臨床驗證走向實質營收
兌現。隨著適應症拓展、區域市場放量、複合式療法推進至一線治療，禾榮科的全
球商業模式將加速複製，在設備銷售與藥物收入隨病患數持續堆疊下，營收與獲利
將迎來跳躍式增長，建議買進，目標價以 NPV 估算(WACC=6%，永續性獲利成長
10%)為 825 元。

營運現況與分析 
公司簡介：禾榮科技成立於 2017 年，為一專注加速器型硼中子捕獲治療系統（AB-
BNCT）技術開發與商業化廠商，並以成為 BNCT 領域值得信賴之全球品牌，提供
癌症精準治療，成為 BNCT 全方位解決方案提供者為目標。公司擁有完整研發、製
造與經營管理團隊，成員具多元專業背景，主要股東為泛漢民集團。在資金、營運
到研發管理團隊陣容堅強下，實力強大有利公司在高資本癌症高階醫療設備治療市
場之拓展。 
台灣藥證年底取證在即，1H27 營收正式入帳，獲利加速成長：禾榮科 BNCT 設備
已於 2024 年 11 月取證，硼中子捕獲治療用藥 BPA 藥證申請中，研究部預計今年
底前將取證，1H27 起，隨北榮設備商轉並進行病患收受，南京明碁醫院設備依完
工比例認列營收，及中國醫持續進行相關適應症之臨床試驗與正式治療等多項業務
併進下，營收將進入兌現期，預估 2027 年營收可達 29.6 億元，2028 年在南京明
碁醫院設備續依完工比例認列營收，新加坡、竹銘等國內外 BOT 案接棒商轉挹注
下，預估營收將持續跳增至 41.2 億元(+39.3YoY)，獲利成長列車正式開動。 
圖一：禾榮科 2027 年起將進入營收兌現期

資料來源：禾榮科；永豐投顧研究部整理，Nov. 2026

### Page 2

2

技術領先 x 完整平台 x 生態精準，禾榮科構築 BNCT 行業壁壘：硼中子捕獲治療
（BNCT）相較於傳統重粒子治療（每人平均需 10-20 次），僅需 1 次療程即可
完成，具備顯著的臨床優勢。目前全球除日本住友（Sumitomo）設備已於日本商
轉外，禾榮科的 AB-BNCT 系統也於 2024 年順利取得 TFDA 醫療器材認證，高
居全球開發進度第二快之廠商，且相較住友及其餘臨床中競品，禾榮科 AB-BNCT
為高效率超熱中子產生系統，質子轉換效率(Proton Efficiency)達 4.8，為競爭對手
4 倍以上，質子電流與功率最低，運轉成本亦為最低，由於中子產生效率高，治療
室中殘留的輻射劑量低，只需 10 分鐘待核種衰變後即可進行下位病人治療，一天
治療人數可達 6 人以上，使用效率顯著優於 Sumitomo，可支撐多適應症商業化
與治療量規模化外，禾榮科致力提供以加速器型硼中子捕獲治療（AB-BNCT）為
核心的一條龍整體解決方案，公司橫跨「治療前精準評估、治療中高效計畫與定位、
標靶照射治療、治療後長效維護」四大階段，全面構築競爭對手難以跨越的技術與
行業壁壘。 
跨足全球與多元適應症為禾榮科中長期營運創造強大堆疊效應：目前單台 BNCT 設
備年治療容量可達 1,500 人，以台灣 BNCT 潛在市場年新增病患來約 5.5 萬人，
五年累積人數預估達 24 萬人，復發率 30-40%，設備需求量達超過 50 台，禾榮
科自 2024 年醫療器材取證以來，恩慈治療上已累 300 名以上病患，涵蓋近 30 項
腫瘤適應症，並在復發型頭頸癌、三陰性乳癌等多個適應症上展現優越療效，成功
吸引國內外指標性醫院展開深度合作。研究部認為 2026 年底取證後，2027 年起
BNCT 有機會以多重適應症收治病人。 
台灣市場：除已開展治療的中醫大及 1H27 預計商轉的北榮外，新竹竹銘與義大醫
院也預計於 2028 年接棒營運。此外，國內大型指標教學醫院正與公司進行乳癌臨
床試驗，未來可望深化合作範疇。 
海外市場：佈局正全面加速，包括日本合作方已有病患來台接受治療，東南亞市場
以新加坡為核心，除已於今年五月提出醫材註冊申請，公司近日也公告將以 13.8
億元入主新加坡上市公司 SAM Holdings(SGX：9G2)，取得 50.09%控制權，因
SAM 在新加坡擁有先進之質子治療中心與光子放射治療服務，雙方結合後，禾榮
科將導入自家加速器型硼中子捕獲治療（AB-BNCT）系統，打造東南亞首座「雙
粒子癌症治療平台」，預計 2028 年以醫療中心模式啟動治療，提供全方位精準腫
瘤治療服務，因新加坡具備高度國際化與健全的醫療法規體系，是連結東南亞醫療
市場的關鍵門戶，故未來將以新加坡為樞紐，全力搶攻東南亞與國際醫療商機，美
國市場則已與邁阿密、明尼蘇達、紐約三地指標醫院接洽，並於六月向美國 FDA 
圖二：禾榮科在行業中已建立強勁的技術壁壘

資料來源：禾榮科；永豐投顧研究部整理，Nov. 2026

### Page 3

3

提出上市前諮詢申請。 
整體而言，禾榮科將自 2027 年起迎來營運轉捩點，正式從臨床驗證走向實質營收
兌現。隨著適應症拓展、區域市場放量、複合式療法推進至一線治療，禾榮科的全
球商業模式將加速複製，在設備銷售與藥物收入隨病患數持續堆疊下，營收與獲利
將迎來跳躍式增長。 
2028 年達國產國造目標，設備交期有效掌握下，對業務發展將如虎添翼：禾榮科
AB-BNCT 治療系統目前 90%之子系統由禾榮科自主開發，為全面掌控設備交期、
供應鏈管理與確保關鍵材料之供應，禾榮科與 Advanced Cyclotron Systems Inc.
簽署加速器技術授權移轉案，並於 10/16 公告於苗栗購地打造高階醫療加速器研
發製造與藥物製造基地，整合設備製造、系統組裝、測試驗證與藥物開發能力，預
計 2028 年達到「國產國造」目標。研究部認為，相較於競爭者多以單一產品切入
市場，禾榮科具備「AB-BNCT 設備＋硼中子治療藥物＋治療計劃系統」的完整整
合能力，能為醫療院所提供一站式解決方案，形成明顯的差異化優勢。未來隨國產
自製設備問世，在設備建置時程可有效掌控的前提下，將有望進一步加速全球設備
的鋪設進度與國際業務拓展。 
財務預估：禾榮科在營收結構上，藥物部分將依病患數按比例收取治療費，設備則
採賣斷、BOT 與租賃等多元模式有不同認列方式，多管齊下加速全球布局並建立
可複製的癌症診療平台。獲利部分，由於藥物收入與治療收入的毛利率達 70-80%，
設備認列的毛利率約 60-70%，後續隨著藥物與治療比重增加，獲利率可望提升。 
預估禾榮科 2026 年營收 0.1 億元，稅後虧損 3.98 億元，稅後 EPS-2.52 元，2027
年在北榮設備可完全認列收入，中醫大及北榮開始進行正式病患治療收費，與南京
明碁醫院設備依完工比例認列營收下，預估營收將跳增至 29.6 億元，稅後淨利 16.1
億元，稅後 EPS 10.2 元；隨合作醫院持續進行設備商轉，預估 2028 年竹銘醫院、
義大醫院與新加坡 NUH 可望開始收治病患，營收預估達 41.2 億元(+39.3YoY)，
稅後淨利 22.1 億元(+39.4%YoY)，稅後 EPS 13.94 元；2029 年預估裝機醫院陸續
商轉以及病患持續累積，經常性收入占比擴大，營收 53.9 億元(+30.8%YoY)，稅
後淨利 27.5 億元(+24.8%YoY)，稅後 EPS 17.39 元，後續每年隨著醫院設備裝機
與治療人數累積，獲利可望呈現每年成長。禾榮科的全球商業模式將加速複製，在
設備銷售與藥物收入隨病患數持續堆疊下，營收與獲利將迎來跳躍式增長，建議買
進，目標價以 NPV 估算(WACC=6%，永續性獲利成長 10%)為 825 元。 
圖三：禾榮科全球市場拓展狀況

資料來源：禾榮科；永豐投顧研究部整理，Nov. 2026

### Page 1

2026.11.02   
기아 (000270)

26.3Q Review: 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요  Company Brief

26.3Q Review: 매출액 31.6조원, 영업이익 1.8조원  
기아는 26.3Q 매출액 31.6조원 (+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원 
(+24.3% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, +5.8% OPM) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 26.3Q 영업이익은 
시장 컨센서스 (2.36조원)를 22.9% 하회했다 [iM증권 추정치: 매출액 31.5조원, 
영업이익 2.3조원]. 주요 구성 요소별 내용은 아래와 같다. 
① P: 26.3Q 26.3Q 전동화 차량 판매는 30.0만대 (+47% YoY, 판매 비중 
36.2%)를 기록했는데, 이 중 HEV 17.2만대 (+46% YoY), EV 12.3만대 
(+75% YoY)로 구성됨. 미국 HEV 판매가 전년 동기 대비 118% 증가하며 
시장 성장률을 약 4배 상회했고, 특히 서유럽 EV 판매가 전년 대비 163% 
증가함. 단 26.3Q 중 원화가 빠르게 강세로 전환되며 원/달러, 원/유로 
평균 환율은 전분기 대비 각 5.2%, 6.2% 하락함에 따른 – 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매는 84.7만대 (+7.9% YoY)를 기록했고, 북미, 유럽, 
인도 등 주요 시장에서 고른 성장 나타남. 26.3Q 소매 판매 또한 82.9만대 
(+6.9% YoY)로 글로벌 산업 수요 (-6% YoY)를 상회. 북미는 HEV, 내수와 
유럽은 EV, 인도는 신형 셀토스와 소넷을 중심으로 판매가 확대됨 (이하 
26.3Q 권역별 도매 판매 YoY, 국내: +2.2%, 북미: +7.6%. 유럽: +19.3%. 
인도: +38.5%). 이란 사태 장기화로 아중동 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 
28.4% 감소했으나, 수요가 견조한 중남미와 CIS로 물량을 전환하며 
감소분을 상당 부분 만회함. 연간 도매 판매 가이던스 (335만대)를 유지함. 
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 약 0.7조원으로 추정되며, 전년 동기 대비 
5,000억원 감소함 (25.2Q 0.7조원, 25.3Q 1.2조원, 25.4Q 1.0조원, 26.1Q 
0.8조원). 반면 인센티브 증가 및 가격 인하 7,270억원, 서유럽 EV 비중 
확대에 따른 믹스 악화 4,650억원, 원자재 가격 상승 영향 3,680억원 등 
총 1.6조원의 감익 요인이 발생함. 이에 26.3Q 매출원가율은 84.1%로 
전년 동기 대비 3.0%p 상승함 (인센티브 +1.9%p, 재료비 +1.2%p). 다만 
26.3Q 기말 환율 급락하며 판매보증충당부채 환입 규모가 축소되었고 
판매보증비 약 9,000억원 감소. 판매관리비율은 10.1%로 3.7%p 개선됨. 
26.3Q 기아 순이익은 1.29조원으로 전년 동기 대비 9.6% 감소했다. 원화 강세에 
따라 외환환산 관련 이익이 전년 동기 540억원 이익에서 3,718억원으로 손실 
전환됐고, 현대모비스 등 관계사 이익 감소로 지분법손익 또한 360억원 감소했기 
때문이다 (현대차 순이익 감소 논리와 동일). 1) 산업 수요 감소와 중동 판매 차질에도 
권역별 파워트레인 대응을 통해 그룹사 대비 가지고 있는 외형 성장성을 증명했으나, 
2) 유럽 EV 판매 확대 과정에서 발생한 인센티브와 믹스 악화라는 비용 부담, 원자재 
가격 상승이 관세 감소 효과를 대부분 반납했고, 3) 분기 중 급격한 원화 강세가 
더해지며 시장 기대를 크게 하회하는 수익성을 기록했다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다.

기아 (000270) 
2

실적 발표 후 주가 급락: 성장에도 불구하고 수익성에 대한 우려  
26.10.30 실적 발표 당일 기아 주가는 11.7% 하락했다. 하루 전 발표된 그룹사 실적 
발표와 상반된 모습이었다. 시장이 이번 실적에서 확인한 우려는 아래와 같다. 첫째, 
성장과 이익의 괴리다. 관세 영향을 제외한 26.3Q 영업이익은 2.52조원으로 전년 
동기(2.70조원) 대비 오히려 1,770억원 감소했다. 판매 증가에 따른 물량 효과 
(+4,420억원)와 환율 효과 (+5,290억원)가 발생했음에도, 인센티브와 믹스 악화로 이를 
대부분 반납한 것이다. 기아의 외형 성장 상당 부분이 유럽 EV 판매 증가에서 나오는데, 
해당 시장에서는 중국 EV 업체와의 경쟁으로 공격적인 프로모션이 불가피하다. 26.3Q 
유럽 권역에서의 중소형 EV 판매가 사업 계획을 크게 웃돌며 EV 믹스는 13.1%에서 
14.8%로 확대됐다. 시장은 이를 M/S를 위해 수익성을 훼손하고 있다고 받아들이는 
것이다. 둘째, 당장 26.4Q에도 유의미한 수익성 개선이 없을 것이라는 점이다. 기아는 
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 4Q 영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이며, 연초 
제시한 연간 가이던스 (영업이익 10.2조원, +8.3% OPM)에 다소 미달할 것이라고 
밝혔다. 중동 분쟁의 영향이 최소 연말까지 이어지고, 원자재와 물류비 부담 또한 
지속될 것이 회사의 판단이다. 무엇보다 원/달러 환율이 26.2Q 평균 약 1,505원에서 
26.3Q 약 1,430원, 현재 1,320~1,340원 수준까지 하락하며 이에 따른 - 영향이 커질 
수 있다. 셋째, 그룹사와의 이익 체력 비교다. 현대차는 26.3Q 파업과 생산 차질로 도매 
판매가 11.3% 감소했음에도 원가 절감을 통해 영업이익이 5.5% 증가했고, 시장 
기대를 상회했다. 반면 기아는 판매가 7.9% 증가했음에도 영업이익이 컨센서스를 20% 
이상 하회했다. 26.3Q 양사 영업이익 격차는 약 8,600억원까지 벌어진 상황이다.  
단기 노이즈를 제외한다면 기아의 기업 가치는 여전히 높다  
그럼에도 당사는 기아의 EV 전략, 낮은 수익성을 이유로 중장기 기업 가치에 대한 
기대를 낮출 이유는 없다고 판단한다. 첫째, 외부 요인을 걷어내면 본원적인 수익 
창출력은 유지되고 있다. 기아는 26.3Q 영업이익률을 낮춘 외부 요인을 원자재 1.2%p, 
중동 판매 차질 0.7%p, 환율 1.7%p 등 총 3.6%p로 분석했다. 즉 중동 분쟁이 
해소되면 상당 부분이 사라질 일회성 요인이다. 둘째, EV 수익성 개선 경로는 HEV가 
걸어온 길과 같다. 기아는 HEV 역시 도입 초기에는 수익을 내지 못했으나 현재는 ICE에 
버금가는 수익성을 내고 있다며, 26~27년 현재의 EV 전략을 유지하겠다고 밝혔다. 
유럽 ICE 시장은 26.3Q 전년 동기 대비 약 20% 축소된 반면 EV 시장은 50% 이상 
성장하고 있다. 전환기에 스케일을 확보하지 못하면 EV 마진을 개선할 방법도 없다. 
기아는 향후 3년간 배터리, 모터, 히트펌프 등 주요 부품에서 연 10% 수준의 원가 
절감을 추진하고, 2~3년 내 EV를 HEV와 같은 수익성 경로에 올려놓겠다는 계획이다. 
셋째, 중장기 성장 계획을 수정하지 않았다. 기아는 27년 사업 계획이 CID 당시 제시한 
숫자와 큰 차이가 없을 것이며, 중장기 영업이익률 10% 목표도 유지했다. IAA 시행에 
대비해 슬로바키아 공장에서 EV를 전량 생산하고 ICE 국내 수출 방안도 검토 중이다. 
주주환원 측면에서도 TSR 35%를 지키고, 자사주 매입보다 배당을 우선하겠다는 
입장이다. 10/30 종가와 최소 DPS 기준만으로도 배당수익률은 6.3%에 달하고 있다.

기아 판매관리비 (좌) 판매관리비율 (우)
  26.4Q 기아 매출액 31.8조원, 영업이익 2.2조원 전망 
기아 26.4Q 실적을 매출액 31.8조원 (+13.2% YoY), 영업이익 2.2조원 (+20.8% YoY, 
+7.0% OPM)으로 전망한다. 26.4Q 판매 성장세는 이어지고, 3Q에 집중 반영된 원자재 
부담 일부 완화와 계절적 성수기 효과로 3Q 대비 수익성은 소폭 개선될 것이다. 다만 
1) 원화 강세에 따른 평균 환율 하락, 2) 유럽 EV 인센티브의 높은 수준 유지 (미국은 
할부 금리 상승으로 QoQ 약 200달러 증가, 유럽은 전분기와 유사), 3) 중동 판매 
차질과 원자재, 물류비 부담 지속을 감안하면 의미 있는 반등은 어렵다. 이는 4Q 
영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이라는 회사의 언급과도 궤를 같이한다. 
기아 목표주가 150,000원으로 하향  
기아 목표주가를 26.4Q 추정치 하향을 반영해 150,000원으로 하향한다. 기아의 판매 
경쟁력은 금번 분기에도 증명됐다. 다만 EV 판매 확대가 수익성 개선으로 연결된다는 
점이 숫자로 확인되기 전까지, 시장은 기아의 성장에 할인율을 적용할 것이다. 중동 
분쟁 해소와 EV 원가 절감이 맞물리는 27년이 기아가 수익성을 증명할 시점이 될 
것이며, 6%를 상회하는 배당수익률이 그때까지 주가의 하방을 지지할 것으로 판단된다.

당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

Document 288: ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold Panic Worsens

Source PDF: all report pdf/ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold Panic Worsens.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold Panic Worsens
Date:    17 minutes ago
ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold Panic Worsens

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock
Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold
Panic Worsens
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 19 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Gary Meulemans / Unsplash

I. Anthropic Makes History, Stocks Fall
Today marked Anthropic's stock market debut. It showed that enthusiasm
for AI remains strong, even as broader market sentiment has soured.
Anthropic raised $81.3 billion through its IPO, surpassing SpaceX's $75
billion offering to become the largest IPO in history. Including a concurrent

### Page 2

private placement, the deal raised $107.3 billion. The shares were priced
at $83, valuing Anthropic at $1.5 trillion and making it the world's 12th
most valuable publicly traded company. They opened at $111.50, briefly
pushing its valuation above $2 trillion, before retreating from an intraday
high of $115.10 to $95.05, still 14.5% above the IPO price.
The S&P 500 reversed an early rally this morning to fall 2.0%, while the
Nasdaq dropped 2.2% (charts). Not even the largest IPO in history could

reverse the jitters surrounding the AI trade.
The selloff extended to semiconductor stocks, with AMD plunging 7.7%
after a short-seller report raised concerns about its ties to Chinese
entities, including a US-blacklisted server manufacturer (chart). Coming
on the back of the Nvidia controversy and tighter US export controls, the

### Page 3

report provided another reminder of the political speed bumps facing the
AI trade.
For us, however, the IPO's biggest significance lies ahead. We have
argued that financial, political, and physical constraints may slow the AI
buildout, but are unlikely to halt it as long as demand for compute keeps
growing. Anthropic's future earnings reports will provide a clearer window
into AI adoption across the economy, helping us assess whether demand
for compute is strong enough to sustain one of the US economy's most

important stimulus programs: the AI buildout. We believe it is and continue
to view the recent selloff as a buying opportunity.
We are sticking with our S&P 500 target of 7,900, based on projected
2027 EPS of $425 and a forward P/E of 18.6. Industry analysts continue
to raise earnings estimates, with the consensus 2028 EPS estimate at
$496 versus our $450 forecast (chart). We suspect some irrational
exuberance, as these forecasts fail to account for the AI buildout's speed

bumps.

### Page 4

Investors, meanwhile, are increasingly skeptical of the analysts' optimistic
forecasts. So investors pushed the S&P 500 forward P/E down to 17.6
(chart). That's below last year's Liberation Day low. We think that panic
selling should abate soon.
Today's latest stock market selloff suggests that the two Bull/Bear Ratios
we track will continue to fall below their averages over time (since 1987)
this week (chart). From a contrarian perspective, that would be bullish.

### Page 5

II. US Capital Flows Supporting The Dollar
The latest Treasury International Capital (TIC) data show that foreign
demand for US financial assets remained exceptionally strong through
September. Net foreign capital inflows totaled $1.43 trillion over the 12
months through September, remaining near the record-high $1.82 trillion
last year (chart).
Equities attracted a staggering $944.7 billion, including $787.1 billion from
private foreign investors and $157.6 billion from foreign official institutions

(chart).

### Page 6

The 12-month total for foreign equity inflows has surged over the past two
years to around $1.0 trillion on a 12-month basis (chart).
Private foreign purchases of US equities reached $787.1 billion, nearly
matching the $821.5 billion pouring into US bonds (chart).

### Page 7

Interestingly, corporate bonds attracted more foreign private capital than
Treasuries, underscoring the appeal of US corporate debt (chart).
III. US Economy On A Roll
The robust foreign demand for US financial assets is consistent with the
underlying strength of the US economy. The Atlanta Fed's GDPNow
model estimates Q3 real GDP growth at a solid 3.2% (saar). Consumer
spending is expected to grow at a robust 2.5% pace, while business
investment is projected to surge 9.2% (chart).

### Page 8

The labor market remains strong too, with initial jobless claims at just
203,000 and continuing claims at 1.76 million, both near multi-year lows
(chart).
IV. Gold Keeps Falling Through Support Levels
The gold market is experiencing a selling panic. The recent selloff below
$4,000 per ounce, down to $3,682, was accompanied by record increases
in daily volume in the GLD ETF (charts). We recently pushed our $5,000
target from the end of this year to the end of next year. Our conviction

level has been greatly reduced by the selloff since the start of the Middle
East war this year. We expect that the gold price will find support around
$3,400.

### Page 9

may be featured in the next "Just Ask Dr. Ed!"

Document 298: Australia and NZ Weekly 9 November 2026 (2026-11-09)

Date: 2026-11-09

Status: ok

### Page 1

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
1WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIA &
NEW ZEALAND
WEEKLY
Week beginning 9 November 2026
In this week’s edition:
Economic Insight: RBA hikes cash rate to new high of 4.85%, a December pause likely.
The Week That Was: Whiplash.
Focus on New Zealand: No deals are done.
For the week ahead:

Australia: Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment, business survey, dwelling finance approvals, RBA's Hunter
and Baker speaking.
New Zealand: retail card spending, RBNZ's Conway speaking.
China: CPI, PPI, trade balance, current account, new loans.
Euro Area: Q3 GDP, trade balance.
United Kingdom: Q3 GDP, BoE Governor Bailey speaking.
United States: Veterans Day, CPI, PPI, small business optimism, inflation expectations.
Analysis and forecasts for this week’s key releases.

Information contained in this report current as at 6 November 2026.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As expected, the RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) lifted
the cash rate 25bps to 4.85% at its November meeting.
This follows the increase at the previous (late-September)
meeting and is a marked turnaround from the outlook back
in August. The post-meeting media statement cited higher

global energy prices resulting from the Middle East conflict,
tighter domestic capacity pressures and an increase in
inflation expectations for the decision.
However, the decision was a split vote, 6 votes to 3, a
possibility we have flagged for some time. In the post-meeting
media conference, Governor Bullock explained that the
dissenting minority put more weight on the downside risks and
did not see signs of wages growth picking up as much as the

staff forecasts implied. Those members were reportedly more
confident that slower growth would prevent higher inflation
expectations becoming entrenched.
Financial conditions are restrictive and this is expected to
bring inflation down over time. Because the RBA assesses the
risks to the inflation outlook to still be skewed to the upside,
the outlook for coming meetings will still be for a decision
between holding and hiking. But we note that the post-meeting

statement omitted September’s language “including increasing
the cash rate target further if needed” from the commitment to
do what is necessary to get inflation down.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor
commented that after five hikes, this is probably a good time
to “take stock”. Together with the dissents, we read this as
confirming our current view that the MPB will keep rates
on hold in December. There is also considerable data flow

between now and then, which could go either way, including
the Q3 national accounts.
Similarly, a lot could happen between now and February, but
we see it as the next most likely timing for a hike, should one
eventuate. We still see that as a risk rather than a base case.
Revised forecasts published in the accompanying Statement
on Monetary Policy (SMP) show underlying inflation lingering
around current rates for longer. This contrasts with the
previous SMP forecasts in August, which showed trimmed

mean inflation returning to the 2–3%yr target range by late
2027 and a little below the 2.5%yr target midpoint in 2028.
We note, however, that the conditioning assumptions on oil
prices, which are based on futures prices, were at a local peak
at the time the forecasts were finalised.
RBA hikes cash rate to new high of 4.85%, a December pause likely
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The RBA’s GDP growth forecasts for 2027 and 2028 were
both revised down –0.3%pts to 1.3% for 2027 and 1.5% in

2028, despite an upward revision to the near term. This is
well below even the RBA’s downbeat view of potential output
growth. Within the GDP aggregate, recent misses and leading
indicators have required upward revisions to the near-term
outlooks for business and dwelling investment, and downside
revisions to public demand. These revisions were offset further
out with downward revisions to the 2028 outlook for business
and dwelling investment and an upward revision to public

demand. Household consumption forecasts were revised down
across most of the profile.
With the unemployment rate already at 4.7% in August and
September – a rate the RBA did not forecast until end-2027 in
its August SMP forecasts – the RBA has lifted its unemployment
forecast to show a peak of 5.1% in at end-2028, the end of the
forecast horizon. This largely reflects stronger labour supply,
with forecasts for employment growth essentially unchanged.

We have repeatedly flagged that cost-of-living pressures are
likely to keep people in the labour force and thus boost the
participation rate. The RBA had previously not factored this
adequately into its forecasts but acknowledged in the SMP
that this mechanism had kept participation higher than it had
expected at the time of the August SMP.
However, because inflation surprised to the upside recently,
this additional labour supply has had no bearing on the

RBA’s assessment of capacity pressures. The RBA assesses
the domestic economy to still be a little tight, and tighter
than previously judged. The RBA’s framework for assessing
the degree of spare capacity or tightness in the economy
necessarily translates an upside surprise on inflation (or unit
labour costs) into a conclusion that the economy must be
tighter than previously judged. Indeed, the RBA’s estimate
of the NAIRU (unemployment rate consistent with inflation

stable at target) has been revised up to 4.75%.
The RBA highlighted that elevated inflation expectations may
be contributing to higher realised inflation. Consumer inflation
expectations have started to lift again but remain below where
they peaked earlier this year. However, the RBA appears
“RBA lifts cash rate 25bps to 4.85% in split
vote, citing “broadly based” domestic inflation
pressures, Middle East conflict and AI boom."

to be putting more attention on the inflation expectations
of firms and the frequency of mentions of pricing in their
liaison program. This is putting a lot of weight on proprietary
information that is not clearly validated by other, longer-
running data sources.

The AI boom is heavily cited in the SMP and other post-
meeting communication. The RBA has lifted its forecasts
for major trading partners, especially high-income Asian
economies that are the centre of high-tech manufacturing.
Demand for AI-related goods is also cited as a driver of both
global and domestic inflation. And although the RBA has
revised up its near-term forecasts for business investment
on the back of strong data centre activity, it is working on

the assumption that Australia does not become ‘a significant
regional hub for data centres’. Nor does it allow for any boost
to productivity trends from the technology, even in 2028. This
is a very different view of the risks from AI than the RBNZ
expressed last week. For the RBA, it is all about inflation, a
little bit about pressure on domestic construction resources,
and nothing about either an ugly unwind of the current
financing boom or a future productivity dividend.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: whiplash
total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of
Australia’s data-centre investment boom, which should feature
prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3
National Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term

elections. In the House, Senate and a series of State Governor
races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come
2027, the Democrats will have a comfortable majority in both
houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-
of-living pressures. The election result also limits President
Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against
Iran. Notably, both immediately before and after the election,

President Trump and the administration have exhibited a greater
interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not
a surprise that Brent oil has backed off its most recent highs
around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to
near USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since
traded back up to above USD99, still 16% below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a
robust broad-based rally in major equity indexes, concerns

over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related
infrastructure buildout jolted US equities lower overnight – the
Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of
the prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and
FOMC rate expectations have also been scaled back; so, while
tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better
outcomes across the rest of the US economy may become
evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest

their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in
the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s concerns over US inflation
should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not
hike in December is a high bar, but the probability of further
hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate
by 25bps to 4.00% at their November meeting as expected.
On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to

rise from 3.5%yr at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity
data has also surprised to the upside in recent months, though
"there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other
prices and wages".
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase
at its November meeting, taking the cash rate to 4.85%. The
move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued
concern around inflation following the recent run higher in
global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation
is now not expected to return to the mid-point of the
target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth
expectations and the unemployment rate trending up to
5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with
three members preferring to remain on hold given downside
risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth.
Governor Bullock’s suggestion that it was time to “take stock”

after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference
to raising rates further “if needed”. In our view, February
represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but
we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible.
Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in October to now
be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline
is approaching the scale of previous housing corrections but

appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in
September, but this was driven by volatile unit approvals while
detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates
and the established-market downturn are beginning to weigh
on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile
rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3; however, much of
the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly

for fuel. Real spending volumes expanded by a much more
modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at
income, spending and saving flows. The latest update suggests
that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are
increasingly being squeezed, particularly those with low savings
buffers. With another rate increase now delivered, housing
wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like

the high-water mark for growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus
widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-monetary gold trade
yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The
most notable development was another exceptionally strong
result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the Q3
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics

In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the

was a decision that needs to be made in December, but rather
a risk to be judged as additional information is received. This
points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027
than before, and only if conditions / risks warrant.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
No deals are done
This week’s developments underscore our message that
a rate hike at the RBNZ’s December meeting is far from a
done deal. New Zealand’s labour market remains softer
than expected, and that will be noted by those on the

policy committee who question the extent of second-round
inflation pressures. The rout in the US mid-term elections
has also taken significant heat out of oil prices for now,
although it’s unclear whether that situation will hold through
to next month. Our view on the endpoint for this OCR cycle
remains unchanged, but there’s a growing risk that the RBNZ
could slow the pace of tightening in the new year.
The September quarter labour market surveys pointed to

ongoing softness in the jobs market. The unemployment
rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing a four-year run
of increases, and reaching its highest level since 2015.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth
remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-living pressures.
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise
0.1% fall in employment in the September quarter, after a
downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That

went against the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure
of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward momentum
emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure
had outpaced the MEI for the previous nine straight quarters).
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in
the labour force participation rate from 70.6% to 70.4%.
These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly
large increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had

suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error
in the survey rather than a genuine surge.
We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate,
which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message
here is one of softness. Our best interpretation is that, to the
extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the
growth in the population (which itself is a fairly low bar, as net
migration remains at a low ebb).

Even with the economy returning to growth over the last
year or so, hiring activity has remained weak. This may be
an ongoing legacy of employment trends in previous years.
Businesses have told us that they felt burned by the severe
skill shortages that developed over 2021-23, and looked to
hold on to workers to the extent they could during the 2024-
25 downturn. The flipside of this is that they may now find
themselves overstaffed even as activity picks up again.

This shows up most strikingly in labour force transition rates.
The rise in unemployment over the last few years has not
been driven by an increase in job losses, but by accumulation
of people who, once out of work, are finding it increasingly
difficult to get back in again. That also extends to young
people trying to enter the workforce for the first time – youth
unemployment of around 25% is the highest in the history of
the survey going back to 1986.

The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September
quarter, in line with our expectations overall but a bit weaker
in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while
there was a surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector
wages. There was a notable jump in the number of employers
citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay
rates – although the size of those increases varied a great deal,
with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate.

Potential RBNZ implications
The labour market data was unequivocally weaker than
expected by either us or the RBNZ. While likely not a
gamechanger for the cycle overall, it will add ammunition to the
arguments of the doves on the Monetary Policy Committee that
a pause in interest rate rises will make sense soon – perhaps in
December or early next year. It’s likely the case that interest rate
markets were over-pricing the odds of a hike at the 9 December

MPS, so the subsequent pullback in pricing makes sense.
The message from the RBNZ in the October meeting was to
be careful about pricing in "done deals", and that the outlook
Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
will be data-dependent. As we noted at the time, alongside
measures of inflation expectations, the key domestic data
to watch ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December meeting will
be indicators of what is happening to excess capacity (the
output gap), as this will be a key determinant of the Bank’s

assessment of the outlook for inflation over the coming year.
The RBNZ looks at a broad suite of indicators of labour market
slack, although their research found that only four of them
seem to have predictive power: the unemployment rate,
the vacancy to unemployment ratio, the QSBO measure of
labour as the main limiting factor to growth, and the rate of
transition between jobs. The first three of these all pointed
to a softening in the September quarter. (The fourth one is

calculated from income tax data, so it’s not publicly available
and even the RBNZ receives it only with a lag.)
In the eyes of the dovish MPC members, the softness in
the labour market indicators will likely push in the direction
of having greater comfort with the medium-term inflation
outlook. The outcome of the election, with the possibility of a
return to a dual inflation/employment mandate, could increase
the prominence of these factors. This likely won’t be an explicit

factor in the RBNZ’s reasoning at its December review, but it
may be in the minds of individual MPC members.
That said, it’s important to remember that in the RBNZ’s
forecasting framework, the GDP figures are the main
determinant of excess capacity in the economy. The most
recent GDP data tells us that the output gap probably
narrowed a bit through the June quarter, mainly reflecting
historical revisions. In light of the latest labour market figures,

there may be some downside risk to the outlook for Q3 GDP –
but there are conflicting indicators there, so it’s not that clear.
At last month’s surprise on-hold OCR decision, Governor
Breman lamented that there was no protocol for the RBNZ
to be able to signal how it was reading the economic data –
in particular the softer than expected Q3 CPI print – ahead
of the policy meeting. In that light, it will be interesting to
see whether they use some already-scheduled speaking

engagements to signal what they make of the labour market
data. Top of mind is Chief Economist Conway's 10 November
speech to the UBS Australasia Conference (pointedly titled
“How is the NZ economy tracking”). Governor Breman is also
speaking to the Institute of Directors on 19 November.
Our view remains that the December OCR hike is more likely
than not. But there will be a clear debate to be had between
the MPC hawks and doves, and it may again come down to a

vote. We still expect a 4% peak in the OCR for this cycle, but
the time taken to get there could be more flexible. Takes along
the lines of "3% is the terminal rate" seem rather hasty on the
basis of a single data release.
Aside from how the Committee interprets the labour market
data, the key considerations in December will be: the RBNZ’s
surveys of inflation expectations by households and businesses;
whether the US mid-term elections lead to a sustained de-

escalation in the Middle East and a pullback in oil prices; and
whether developments in the AI space exacerbate some MPC
members’ concerns about the global economic environment.
And then of course there’s the result of the General Election,
with voting closing on Saturday evening. The actual formation
of a government is likely to take weeks of negotiation, so the
outcome may not be known by the 9 December MPS but at least
the range of possibilities should be clearer. We’ll publish our

initial thoughts on the election results early on Monday morning.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Oct Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Nov 12, Last: –0.4, Westpac f/c: 1.4
We’re forecasting a 1.4% rise in retail spending in October.
While that would be a solid increase, it’s in large part due to
the 11% rise in petrol prices over the past month. Stripping out

fuel spending, we’re forecasting a 0.6% rise in core spending
categories. Core spending has been very choppy recently,
and the increase we’re assuming follows falls in the past few
months. A result in line with our expectations would leave the
trend in core spending relatively flat since the start of this year
with the rise in fuel prices weighing on spending in other areas.
AUS: Nov Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Nov 10, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment fell 4.7% to 80.4 in Oct, returning to the

deeply pessimistic levels seen earlier in the year. Responses
over the course of the survey week showed a very sharp
deterioration after the RBA rate hike.
The Nov survey is in the field over the week ended Nov 7.
Interest rates will again factor with the RBA raising the cash
rate by another 25bps to 4.85%, an 18yr high. Average pump
prices also pushed higher, the national average just under
$2.40/litre, while the housing market downturn has continued

to broaden and deepen. The US mid-term election results may
have some effects on sentiment locally as well.
AUS: Q3 Housing Finance Approvals (%qtr)
Nov 11 Last: –5.2, Westpac f/c: –10
Market f/c: –8.5, Range: –15 to –5
The total value of housing finance approvals fell The value of
new dwelling finance approvals excl. refinance fell 5.2%qtr,
the largest quarterly drop in three years. Annual growth eased
to 7.3%yr. The weakness was accounted for mainly by lower

lending volumes: loan numbers were down 4.0%qtr (–0.6%yr),
while average loan size also contributed negatively.
Q3 is expected to see an even bigger decline with the full
impact of interest rate rises and tax policy changes showing
through. The value of both loan enquiries and turnover fell by
around 10% in the quarter. We expect total finance approvals
to show a 10% drop as well, with investor loan approvals likely
to be down 16%qtr and owner occupier approvals down 6%qtr.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
US: Oct CPI (%mth)
Nov 11, Headline, Last: 0.6, Market f/c: 0.3, Westpac f/c: 0.4
US consumer prices rose by 0.6% in September, although
half of the gain was due to energy inflation, with core prices
instead up 0.3%. Over the year, prices were reported to have

risen 3.7%yr overall and 2.6%yr excluding food and energy.
Constructively, ex food, energy and shelter, annual inflation
met the FOMC’s medium-term target of 2.0%yr.
Come October, energy price pressures are set to recede
given the pre-election run higher in oil prices came late in the
month. A softer print for shelter is also likely to moderate core
momentum. We see a 0.4% gain for the headline measure and
a 0.2% result for core inflation as most probable. Looking to

2027, disinflation is expected to continue at a measured pace
towards the medium-term target.

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Linh kiện ô tô, Dầu khí và Công nghệ là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -634 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VPL; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-609 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -5,829 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -1,462 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 722 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -5,278 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam đánh mất đà hồi phục của tuần trước, khi VN-Index quay đầu giảm khoảng 
1,8% về đóng cửa quanh 1.742 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm lùi sâu về sát 1.727 
điểm trước khi thu hẹp đà giảm. Dù mùa báo cáo KQKD quý 3 diễn ra tương đối tích cực, 
tuy nhiên các lo ngại về việc môi trường lãi suất cao vẫn duy trì trong bối cảnh căng thẳng 
Trung Đông leo thang đang đè nặng lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Kết quả kỳ họp của FED trong 
tuần qua nhìn chung phù hợp với kỳ vọng và không gây ra ảnh hưởng đáng kể. Điểm kém 
tích cực nhất là khối ngoại đã quay lại bán ròng với tổng giá trị khoảng 2.700 tỷ đồng 
trong tuần — đảo ngược diễn biến mua ròng cuối tuần trước, cho thấy đà rút vốn ngoại 
vẫn chưa thực sự dừng lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến giảm trên đồ thị tuần, nhưng chưa thoái lui quá 1 
nửa cây nến tăng của tuần trước, cùng thanh khoản được tiết giảm, hàm ý nhịp điều chỉnh 
vẫn trong phạm vi cho phép. Trên khung ngày, sau bốn phiên giằng co đầu tuần với thanh 
khoản thấp, chỉ số đã chịu áp lực sụt giảm mạnh hơn trong phiên cuối tuần, chớm xuống 
dưới đường tín hiệu chính giữa dải Bollinger, nhưng cho tín hiệu rút chân ngay sau đó, thể 
hiện áp lực bán tháo đã tạm thời được kiểm soát. Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều 
chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX 
được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

MACRO STRATEGY
AI Implies Dramatic Shift in the Structure of the U.S. Economy
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
On November 16, Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh moderated three conference
sessions at a symposium focused on how AI will ripple through the economy. The event
was the first public look at one of the five task forces Warsh created to review the
Federal Reserve (Fed) processes for doing its job. The task forces are meant to help

reform the Fed by building a consensus for overhaul to address shortcomings Mr. Warsh
has raised about the Fed's performance over the years. The outsized role of AI in the
evolution of the U.S. economy is reflected in the task force to consider AI's impact on
employment and productivity. This is, perhaps, the least controversial of the various
issues the task forces are charged with considering but nevertheless one of major
importance.
The main takeaway from the symposium was that various scenarios remain possible

with quite different implications for the future of U.S. growth, employment and interest
rates. The wide range of outcomes has contributed to the recent volatility in equity
markets, especially the parts of the market most closely associated with the buildout
of AI data centers. Estimating the payoff from the buildout naturally remains a key
focus for investors but one that is subject to uncertainty because of the rapid pace of
technological innovation and the political pressures that are arising from public concerns

over the changes AI is bringing.
Exhibit 1 helps shed light on the dramatic consequences AI is likely to create for the
structure of the U.S. economy and employment. The cost structure of corporate output
as of 2025 includes about half of each dollar for labor. An estimate for the cost structure
of a dollar spent on inferential AI after the data center buildout reaches a net stock
centered around $5 trillion in 2030 requires only a fourth as much labor per unit of

revenue or output. The bigger the share of overall value inferential AI becomes in gross
domestic product (GDP) the lower the labor share of income will become. This is not a
new trend. The labor share of income has been gradually declining ever since the digital
economy began to develop over a half century ago. What is new is the speed of the
transformation. Self-generative AI is representative of the singularity, the point when the
spread of this digital transformation becomes exponential.

The drastically lower labor share of value implied by AI output implies that other costs
comprise bigger shares. For example, the exhibit shows the profit share of revenue is
much higher (28% vs.19%) for AI-generated output. This is already evident in the profit
profile of the S&P 500 where the margins and profit growth of the AI-related companies
are orders of magnitude greater than the profit profiles of non-AI-related businesses.
Presumably, as the old economy increasingly transforms to this new economy cost

structure, the productivity benefits of AI will increasingly transfer over to traditional
businesses and transform their cost structures in the direction of those of inferential AI
builders. This analysis suggests profit growth is moving toward a bigger share of output
over the next few years.
Competition naturally eats away at "excess" profits, those not justified by costs. Exhibit 1
partly illustrates the justification for higher AI profits. AI is capital-intensive compared

to the non-AI economy. This can be seen in the much higher depreciation-cost share
in the AI economy (28% vs.15%). AI capital is more short-lived than average, reflecting
the quicker obsolescence that naturally results from accelerating technological change
and less durable infrastructure (short-lived chips). This issue of rapid depreciation has
surfaced in the debate over potential AI profitability, with analysts trying to figure the
right life span of data centers to model their profit outlook.

Investment Implications
AI's creative destruction is likely to
leave a lot of laggards in its wake while
creating a stronger, more profitable
economy for investors. As a result,
we believe a diversified investment
approach favoring high-quality balance
sheets and reliable free cash flows is
warranted.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Finally, "other costs" make up about a third of AI output cost compared to just 12% for
non-AI output. Among these are the increased power costs associated with intensive
compute processing. Electricity costs are going to replace a lot of labor costs. As the
midterms made clear, rules and regulation for data centers, including power and water
issues, are likely to come under much greater scrutiny as well as other environmental and

"quality-of-life" issues.
Ultimately, this new cost structure for the AI economy will be the focus of innovation
to bring down costs and remove bottlenecks. Technology is ultimately deflationary.
Monetary policy is the cause of inflation. This "deflationary" or declining cost aspect
of technological progress is a key factor behind the notion that the promise of the
singularity is a world of superabundance. Intelligence, power and compute costs are likely

to become cheaper and cheaper as innovators work to create this superabundance.
Exhibit 1: Comparison of Corporate Cost Structure to Produce 2025 Output to
Corporate Cost Structure to Produce Inference Compute Revenue from AI Data
Center Buildout by 2030.

Employment and AI. The dramatic reduction in labor needed to run an AI cost structure
promises a much higher level of productivity and therefore higher levels of wealth and
income. As the cost structure in Exhibit 1 illustrates, however, a much bigger share
of output goes to capital and therefore shareholders in an AI-dominated economic
structure. There are various ways this change could sort itself out. In a society with an
increasingly older population, more capital income solves the problem of more retirees

needing to live on investment income. AI also promises to increase longevity. Therefore,
the future is likely to include more elderly people living on capital income as well as
more elderly people working. In addition, the bounty from higher productivity is likely
to support demand for new goods and services as past technology breakthroughs have
shown. People will be required to produce those new goods and services.
Nevertheless, it's clear from the distribution of capital income that's becoming more, not

less, concentrated, that policies that spread wealth ownership across a broader swath
of the population could potentially help ease the social tensions plaguing the population
under the guise of "affordability." The proliferation of gifts from the wealthy to Trump
Accounts is a reflection of the recognition that this issue needs to be addressed. The
U.S. Treasury rolled out temporary regulations at the end of September that allow Trump
Accounts to hold donated individual stocks instead of just diversified index funds. There

is likely to be a push to expand the growth of large philanthropic donations from the
private sector to fund retirement-type accounts for the broader population. This is an
alternative to higher tax rates that fund government spending rather than long-term
saving. A society that shares more broadly in the fruits of AI is more likely to tolerate AI's
profound changes.

### Page 4

4 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Too Connected to Fail: When the Jetsons Meet the Flintstones
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
This month, when a leading U.S. technology company went dark, planes across America
were grounded, and major banks went offline, investors learned an unsettling lesson on
the fragility of America’s digital economy.  We learned that interconnectivity is both a

strength and a weakness—and that the digital economy of the future rests upon the
shaky infrastructure of the past.
We live in a world where AI is advancing at warp speed. Computing power is expanding
exponentially. Financial transactions move around the globe in milliseconds. And cloud
computing keeps billions of people and businesses connected every day. It is a world
where everything is faster, digital and connected.
The U.S. digital economy now accounts for roughly 12% of GDP, expanding about three

times faster than the economy overall this century. Digital gross output totaled $3.7
trillion in 2021, the last year of available data from the Commerce Department; that said,
the figure almost certainly understates the current size of the U.S. digital economy. Digital
commerce now accounts for nearly 18% of all retail sales ($350 billion), although again,
this number is understated. Add in business-to-business digital transactions and digitally

delivered professional services, and the amount of daily digital commerce in the U.S. is
massive and growing robustly. Each one of these transactions ultimately depends on
communications services, electricity, computing facilities, and digital payments networks.
Yet here’s the rub: our digital world is juxtaposed against a physical infrastructure
of aging radar systems, antiquated telecommunications equipment, half-century-old
transmission lines, vulnerable underwater subsea cables and decades-old software.

Here we are, well into the 21st century, and a handful of Federal Aviation Administration
facilities are still using floppy disks to support aviation travel. Our world is a world where
the space-age Jetsons have collided with the Stone Age Flintstones.
Something had to give, and it did this November: business communications were
disrupted, planes were grounded, ATMs were frozen and investors were left wondering
“what’s next”?
In our opinion, “what’s next” entails a renewed focus and commitment on the part of the

U.S. public and private sectors to build America’s physical infrastructure back better and
faster. The recent disruptions, we believe, are a reminder that a nation’s infrastructure is
essentially its backbone, and every nation, like every human being, is only as strong as its
skeletal framework.
A healthy backbone allows an economy to move, pivot, grow, innovate and compete. An
impaired spine, in contrast, constrains mobility, undermines nimbleness and flexibility and

is prone to setbacks.
So, think of roads, bridges, ports, airports, electrical grids, communication networks and,
increasingly, digital networks not merely as conveniences of modern life. Rather, think
of them as foundational components of economic activity, national security and global
competitiveness. Without them, even the world’s most sophisticated economy can be
brought to a standstill.
The great irony—and potential opportunity—of the “too connected to fail” is that while the

U.S. leads the world in AI, cloud computing, and other advanced technological capabilities,
the physical infrastructure of the nation continues to show its age. Electrical grids are
struggling to accommodate increasing demand. Transmission networks are plagued with
bottlenecks. Aviation communications systems built in the 1970s are overwhelmed.
None of the above is new—America’s infrastructure deficiencies have been widely
acknowledged but frequently tolerated for decades. The grid held up most of the time,

while airline safety remained relatively stellar. Power outages during storms and heat
waves were expected and tolerated—no matter the misery created. All good.
Investment Implications
The future is digital, which
requires more capital investment
on America’s aging physical
infrastructure. We continue to favor
Industrials, infrastructure-related
firms, and commodities for the long-
run.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Today, however, it’s different. Because there is so much riding on our digital
connectivity—think trillions of dollars of daily commerce—the cost of failure or
disruption continues to soar. Case in point: America’s recent aviation meltdown. The
communications malfunction triggered a national four-hour ground stop, resulting in
nearly 20,000 flight cancellations over four days, at a potential cost to the airlines

of more than $1 billion in profits. Adding more salt to the wound, fried and frizzled
passengers absorbed an estimated $1.9 billion in additional economic costs. All because
the communications infrastructure supporting the world’s most sophisticated aviation
system failed to handle a technical upgrade.
Where are the Jetsons when you need them? We believe the recent technological failures
will accelerate infrastructure spending for a number of reasons. One, as shown above,

the economic costs of digital failures are rising. Two, America’s digital economy is now
too big—and too connected—to fail. Three, the extraordinary power needed to run
the AI revolution makes it absolutely essential that the U.S. stop talking about fixing
its aging infrastructure and start doing it. Four, with the stakes so large for America’s
largest technology companies, with enormous incentives of their own, these firms are
contributing their private capital of their own to build back America’s infrastructure

faster and better. And fifth, an aging air traffic control system, an unreliable national
grid, and weathered transmission lines―these are more than just economic and financial
challenges. They are challenges to America’s national security—notably to the great
power rivalry between the U.S. and China. This rivalry, in our opinion, will help create
more bipartisan support for increased infrastructure spending and greater public-private
sector cooperation.

Investment summary. It’s taken a series of technological snafus, but here we are—
America’s infrastructure is gaining the attention—and capital—it needs. It’s also
garnered the attention of Congress. Just last week, the Bipartisan American Affordability
Act, which reduces permitting burdens on a number of projects, passed a key procedural
vote in the Senate. Whether the House votes in favor of the bill remains to be seen but
in general, the U.S.’s cumbersome permitting and regulatory systems are coming under

more scrutiny.
To this point, the bipartisan National Governors Association recently outlined
recommendations designed to cut through the permitting and regulatory red tape. The
Department of Energy has developed a coordinated federal transmission permitting
framework designed to clear and approve projects at a faster pace. Yes, it will take
some time to cut through the labyrinth of federal, state and local approvals, permits, and
regulations. But the need for reforms has become quite evident and urgent, with political

inaction less acceptable to an electorate increasingly worried and wary of the next
technology glitch.
So, who potentially benefits in our theme of “too connected to fail”?  In our opinion,
the winners will include electrical equipment manufacturers, engineering firms, utilities,
telecommunications infrastructure providers, industrial automation companies, and
specialized construction firms. Specialists in maritime surveillance, underwater sonar
detection, and fiber-optics sensing capabilities are well positioned in this new world.

In terms of asset allocation, this means considering more exposure to infrastructure-
related industrial companies and leaders in renewables (solar, wind, electric vehicles,
biomass) and the required infrastructure behind each renewable energy source. Leaders
in electricity distribution, charging stations, batteries, as well as low-carbon hydrogen,
biomethane, and advanced biofuels should also be considered in portfolios. Ditto for
leaders in low-carbon technologies (LED lighting, smart energy meters, and storage),

and leaders in transmission technologies like high-voltage direct current (HVDC), which
transfers wind and solar power from where it is created to where it is needed.  We also
still favor commodities over the long term, as well as cybersecurity leaders.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 23, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
In our view, what does the market want and need to see to establish a firmer base?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
IInvestors are looking to reprice uncertainty for 2027. A lot has been digested by this
market since semiconductor stocks peaked in June. Inflation, Fed policy, geopolitics,
the funding of the AI buildout, CEO changes and future power adjustments out of the

midterm elections all in a matter of months.
Most importantly, investors kept clinging to the base—earnings and profitability. As long
as the base remained strong and growing, investors were willing to take the risk of a
market led by technology and the AI buildout more specifically.
Recently, investors have shifted to wanting more certainty: certainty around higher-
quality, transparency in FCF, the ability to secure financing if needed, reasonable

valuations and strong moats that last beyond peak profitability. This is why we have
experienced a shift from the more highly cyclical semiconductors to the hyperscalers in
terms of positioning.
The market wants to see that AI spending remains robust but with more reasonable
expectations for 2027 and perhaps 2028. A reset of expectations by the leaders of
the buildout can go a long way to finalizing this “flush” stage, in our view. We remain
diversified Equity and are proud Bulls overall.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading
companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is the equal-weight version of the widely-used S&P 500, in which each constituent company is given the same weight (approximately 0.2% each), rather than
weighting by market capitalization.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

頂 業 務 ， 提 高 資 本 運 用 效 率 ， 共同分擔

3.5兆美元。這項巨型潛在交易，除了要跨過
極高表決門檻外，SpaceX具極敏感的美國國
防及NASA合作背景，而特斯拉有超過20%
業務倚重上海超級工廠，合併存在嚴重地緣
衝突。預期馬斯克將在川普離任前，加速執
行Tesla與SpaceX 合併。

Tesla財報好壞參半，跑車貢獻有限，潛在合併面臨地緣考驗

投資焦點
► 今年9月適逢地緣擾動持續推升高油價、聯準
會重啟 升息 、財政部與企 業發債 浪潮 等影
響，形塑債市今年以來單月最差表現。檢視
2021年以來美國投資級債前十大單月最差總
報酬，2022年聯準會3月啟動升息年度就佔
一半以上，顯示利率風險仍為近5年來債市最
大風險，然檢視單月最差報酬表現未來期間
總報酬，除2022年1月和3月聯準會重啟升息
前夕與當月表現明顯較弱，多數於未來1年後
多呈現正報酬，反映隨利率走升放大孳息效
益，同時挹注債券長期持有之報酬空間。
► 今年來債券市場利率波動明顯推升信用債利
差，其 中僅 以低 信 評債利 差升幅 較顯 ，此
外，9月以來利率連動度高及AI相關產業債種
也成為利差擴大重災區，凸顯債市投資人減
輕市場不確定性的投資傾向，考量信用債市
歷經利率上行利空洗禮，信用利差同步擴大

創造收益空間，可把握債信體質仍佳企業債
鎖利良機，並透過時間複利力量強化收益。
利率波動升，僅低信評利差擴大程度相對明顯9月為美國投資級債今年最差單月報酬
用時間換空間，單月最差表現形塑未來報酬機會

### Page 8

8
► 受聯準會重啟升息影響，過去一年美國公債
殖利率曲線持續陡峭化，其中利率最大升幅
落在短天期的2至3年期間，美國核心通膨與
就業仍具韌性，聯準會將持續保持緊縮貨幣
政策步調來應對高通膨，考量美國高物價與
高利率環境短期難消，宜透過縮短債券配置
天期並進行動態調整，為因應後續利率不確
定性環境的良策。
► 為因應利率不確定性，投資人可採取債券梯
的投資配置，主要選取不同到期期間及不同
類型債劵進行組合，債券梯投資重點主要在
於，根據殖利率曲線型態及利率動態建立不
同期間債券梯，假設市場利率走升且殖利率
曲線正斜率，建立短天期債券梯除了降低利
率風險，有 短期 閒 置資金 及孳息 需求 投資
人，亦可取得穩定孳息收益。若後續投資人
有短期資金需求，可以視情況將短期債券到
期後資金回收，降低只投資單一債券須持至
到期或直接變現的流動性風險。
投資焦點
美國公債殖利率曲線持續為正斜率
高利率波動持續，用策略迎戰市場擾動
資料來源：Bloomberg，凱基證券整理

經過第2年第一檔債券到期，視資金需求及市況與利率動
態決定後續配置

短天期債券梯：假設買入2檔各3、4、5及7年到期債券，
聚焦短期穩定收益
► 債券梯是一種分散投資方式，主要選取不同到期期間及不同
類型債劵進行組合，可兼具：
1) 分散利率風險：將資金分別買進不同到期年期的債券，以分
散利率風險。
2) 分散信用風險：分散投資不同發行機構債券，不會重壓單一
債券並可分散信用違約風險。
3) 降低流動性風險：若投資人有短期資金需求，可以視情況將
短期債券到期後資金回收，降低只投資單一債券須持至到期
或直接變現之流動性風險。
